All 2,026 verdict-C cross-address classes left after T4's pilot now use the SHARED_FN param-include form: one class header each, included at every old copy under that copy's own name. No class was rejected and none were added to config/dedup_exceptions.tsv, so the R92 stop rule is met at 0 left and there are no buckets to bring to the developer.

The plan's verify command (`share_census.py --check --scope all --strict-macros --strict-text --out-dir .run/3.42/t5/census`) still exits 1. How it ran:
- **Cycle fix:** `tools/share_body_cycle.sh` used to commit with a plain `git commit`. It now commits through `commit_task.sh`, with the task id in `$TASK` and explicit src/ and config/ paths (commit 89da019951). The change is written up in docs/ops/p342-t5-2026-11-08-share-cycle-commit.md.
- **Batches:** 17 batches, cross2 to cross18 (commits 05a5d99816 to 02d5ac206b), ran unattended in one detached cycle run. Each was green on the first try at 538 to 634 s.
- **Checks:** R22 ran after every second batch and was 218/218 each time, including at the last batch's commit 02d5ac206b. I checked that log, and the final tools-health log shows OK.
- **Violations:** shared-source (S1) violations at scope all fell from 3,667 to 1,641, exactly the 2,026 classes converted.
- **Ledgers:** the lever, lying and verbatim ledgers had no rows to re-key, and no repair was needed. The line-shift re-key for `KEPT(REINTERP)` entries is still untested, because the restruct ledger holds none.
- **Cosmetic:** the cycle cuts commit subjects at 100 characters, so they end mid-word ("reje"). I left it unfixed.

For T6: `TASK=T6.c<k> bash tools/share_body_cycle.sh START END 2 cross`, with labels starting at cross19. The cross bucket picks up only verdict C today, so D classes need T6's own handling. A batch takes about 9.3 minutes.

## 114301-028r-claude-answer

ts: 2026-11-09T18:43:01.708Z

---

The coder is fine. The task is slow, but it is making steady progress and nothing is stuck.

**What the 285-minute shell is.** It isn't a single command. It's a loop script that works through batches 21 to 40 of the shared-function conversion, one batch after another. For each batch it:
- converts up to 120 function families to the shared form;
- rebuilds every binary those families touch and checks that each one is still byte-identical;
- commits the batch if everything is green.

The loop stops on the first failure, or when no families are left. The shell's clock covers the whole loop, which is why the number keeps growing.

**What it has done so far:**
- **Batch 21** committed at 10:09. It was a big one: 7,719 sites in 1,783 source files, with 194 of 194 binaries green. That batch alone took about 3 hours, so most of the 285 minutes went there.
- **Batch 22** committed at 10:57: 596 sites, 147 of 147 binaries green. After it, the full rebuild check passed on all 218 binaries.
- **Batch 23** has been running for 42 minutes. Right now it is in the type check, which is busy (one full CPU core, about 4.5 GB of memory).

**Why it's slow.** A batch's time depends on how many files it touches. A wide batch like 21 has to rebuild nearly the whole game. I can't tell you how many batches are left, because the loop only finds out it is done when the pool comes up empty.

I'm keeping an eye on one thing. Batches 21 and 22 between them rejected 50 families, meaning those wouldn't take the shared form byte-identically. Under the rule you picked (0 left), they'll be grouped by reason and brought to you before the conversion counts as done. That's expected. It isn't a problem yet.

I haven't touched anything, and I've released the talk hold.

  - A type-header edit must belong to every binary's bisect set, or a non-main binary stays red after the bisect.

PA3 is updated from 3.32.14 to 3.32.15. It's a hotfix, mainly for busy projects like this one:

- **The mod stays on.** On a busy project, PA3's mod could switch itself off seconds after starting. It re-read a whole month's journal index after every message, and Claude Code stopped it as stuck. The index is gone now, and any old index file is deleted at the next roll-up.
- **Busy days aren't lost.** A finished day's journal over 4 MB used to be skipped. So a fresh Claude, or a restarted task's expert, could miss that day's conversation and notes. A small reader now reads it.
- **"Remember X?" reaches further.** When the records have nothing on a topic, I can now search the raw journal, including talk from before the records began.
- **Small fixes.** `/pa-bugreport` shows the mod version actually loaded. `.claude/pa.json` now tracks the current PA3 version. A few agent files get a one-line version update.

Project Architect 3.32.15, a hotfix (Busy projects keep their mod, and busy days stay in reach), is installed.

**To load it: /exit, then claude --continue (not /clear)**

The mod's own files changed, so no task starts until you restart. T6's question waits too: 729 function families wouldn't take a shared text. After the restart, I'll bring you those groups by reason, with my recommendation.

## 202959-002r-claude-answer
---
ts: 2026-11-10T03:29:59.176Z

---

**Project Architect 3.32.15 is installed and loaded. Ready to continue work.**

T6 needs your decision. Under the 0-left rule you picked, it stopped and grouped the families it couldn't convert.

**Where T6 stands.** It moved 912 of the 1,641 remaining function families to the shared form. These are functions copied at several addresses, and each family now uses one source text included at every copy. Every batch rebuilt byte-identical on all its binaries. Along the way it deleted 59 struct types that nothing used anymore. The full rebuild is 218 of 218, and `tools-health` is OK.

**Why 729 families are left.** For each of them, every candidate text broke something in at least one copy. Here are the reasons, measured per family:

| Reason | Families | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| Conflicting types | 217 | The copies use different struct types for the same data, so one text won't compile everywhere. |
| Return-type lie | 204 | The shared text's return type would disagree with what callers expect somewhere. That's 12,481 call sites. |
| Undeclared data | 160 | The text names a global that some overlay doesn't declare. Cheap to fix by adding the declaration. |
| Dead struct type | 89 | Picking a text would leave a struct type the type check tracks with no users. |
| Unreadable compiler error | 28 | The tool couldn't parse the error. This is a tool gap, not a real wall. |
| Smaller groups | 31 | Argument count 11, void value 9, extern mismatch 6, other 5. |

The 729 is an upper bound. After one failed batch, a family was skipped, so some of these failed only because a neighbour in the same batch broke.

**My recommendation: A.** Run one repair round on the cheap groups: add the missing declarations for the 160, then retry the 28 unparsed families and the skipped ones with the parser fixed. Whatever still fails gets ledgered in `config/dedup_exceptions.tsv`, one row per family with its reason. Then the full-scope check is switched on in `tools-health` with those exceptions allowed.
- **What's left needs other work.** Most of it, the conflicting types and return lies, can't be fixed by picking a better text. It needs struct and declaration cleanup first, which is real work of its own.
- **Ledgering keeps them from being lost.** Each exception would be visible and counted, not hidden.

**What A changes.** The milestone's "0 left" becomes "0 left, or ledgered with a measured reason". That's the same amendment we made for return-type lies in 3.41.

**The other choices:**
- **B** ledgers all 729 now, without the repair round. It's faster, but about 190 families that are probably cheap to fix get ledgered anyway.
- **C** leaves the full-scope check off this phase and hands all 729 to 3.43 unrecorded. I'd avoid it.

The picker is open.

## 235347-508r-T7-interim
---
ts: 2026-11-10T06:53:47.910Z
session: 252d32b5-eb3b-4ca8-9931-8e47bb99cd41
agentId: a05b95d526ad9f91b
role: pa-expert
task: T7
---

done

T7 is finished. Each of the 20 trampoline families now has one source, src/shared/ov/<F>.h, included at all 141 positions where a copy used to sit. Every gate is green.

- **The parameter is a data symbol, not the callee.** The plan expected the copies to differ in the function they wrap. I compared every copy and found the wrapped calls identical everywhere. What differs is one per-overlay data symbol, which appears at 4 places in each copy. So the header takes that symbol as `SHARED_SLOT`, turned into a string inside the asm. `SHARED_FN` stays the name parameter; it is the same in every copy because all copies sit at one address. This is recorded under Deviations in the summary. The done-when itself is unchanged.
- **The conversion.** The coder (T7.c1, commits 13c6f9ab64 and 4abf4deb7a) rewrote 2,720 sites and removed the 20 old partial `F__<h8>.h` headers.
  - **Signatures:** each family takes its majority signature. A few binaries had `(void)` declarations that conflicted; these were changed to match, which leaves the bytes unchanged because the bodies are all asm.
  - **Registry:** the 20 groups in config/dedup.us.yaml now use `tier: h_text`. The `h_exact` form the plan pointed to holds one hash per group, and each family has 134.
  - **Manifest:** the 20 rows now show `private_tus` 0 with the new header path. `--update` added no new rows. The func_80128678 and func_80155FF8 rows are unchanged.
- **Two tools needed to understand the new form.**
  - **The coder's fix:** tools/verbatim_check.py now names a `SHARED_FN` header by its file stem.
  - **What stopped the coder:** tools-health failed on its second cause, a tools/lever_census.py check it could not pass without a brief.
  - **My fix:** I made two small edits to that file. It now parses `SHARED_STR(x)` between string literals, and names those header bodies by file stem so their manifest rows exempt them. The asm count went from 899 to 879 with 0 unattributed.

Results:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `verbatim_check --strict` | no drift |
| `make tools-health` (run t7_health3) | OK |
| R22 (coder's run, after the conversion) | 218/218 |
| `share_census --scope all` | 0 violations before and after |
| `dedup_integrate --check` | 6,275 groups, 0 failed |
| `type_census --check` | OK, every lying row ledgered |

R22 was not re-run after my edit, since it touched no build input. The technique is banked as cookbook/C0559.md. There are no open questions.
